([relexlegal/relex-claude](https://github.com/relexlegal/relex-claude/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/relex-matter))
- **relex-ontology**: Use when working a Relex case beyond a single question — auditing what the case "understands", finding gaps or contradictions in the case/firm ontology graph, and deciding what to acquire next (RAG, statutes, case law, documents). Teaches the read → audit → repair → direct-acquisition → converge loop over the Relex MCP. ([relexlegal/relex-claude](https://github.com/relexlegal/relex-claude/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/relex-ontology))
- **relex-participants**: WHO is on a Relex case — sealed legal parties vs app participants (lawyers, staff, guests, partners), always as labels, never names. Essential when working a case from a shared Slack channel with Claude tagged in. Teaches who's-who, the two never-joined name-spaces, and channel-to-case binding. ([relexlegal/relex-claude](https://github.com/relexlegal/relex-claude/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/relex-participants))
- **relex-partner**: Guide a lawyer or firm through joining the Relex partner program — the registration that lets them charge their own clients (fees, invoices) via their own Stripe account. Covers the states (pending, verified, published), payment onboarding, Relex verification, and when client invoicing unlocks. ([relexlegal/relex-claude](https://github.com/relexlegal/relex-claude/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/relex-partner))
- **relex-research**: Use when a Relex case needs legal authority — statutes, case law, regulator guidance, local ordinances — in ANY jurisdiction. Teaches the division of labour — you DISCOVER authorities with your own search and public legal MCPs; the Relex harness FETCHES-AND-CACHES verbatim text so drafts can cite it — plus per-jurisdiction channels and compliance limits. ([relexlegal/relex-claude](https://github.com/relexlegal/relex-claude/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/relex-research))
- **relex-steering**: Use whenever substantive Relex case work must be produced — drafting, research, re-reasoning, or any multi-step task on an active case. You produce the legal reasoning yourself. Relex files the document, fetches official text, and keeps the anonymized case. Also covers eval-mode restraint and the support-not-admin rule. ([relexlegal/relex-claude](https://github.com/relexlegal/relex-claude/tree/HEAD/plugin/skills/relex-steering))
- **reliability**: Run one analytics task through several fresh trials to measure what repeats and what varies. Use for reliability, repeatability, variance, or repeated-run requests. This measures stability, not correctness. ([ai-analyst-lab/ai-analyst](https://github.com/ai-analyst-lab/ai-analyst/tree/HEAD/.claude/skills/reliability) · ★328)
- **reliability**: Check whether an AI analysis answer is STABLE by running the same question several independent times and measuring what holds versus what drifts. Trigger on "/reliability", "run this a few times", "is this answer stable / reliable", "check reliability of &lt;question&gt;", "does it give the same answer again", "run it N times and compare". The cheapest eval and the only one needing no answer key: stability, not correctness. ([ai-analyst-lab/ai-analyst-plugin](https://github.com/ai-analyst-lab/ai-analyst-plugin/tree/HEAD/ai-analyst-plus/skills/reliability) · ★34)
- **reliability**: Site reliability engineering -- SLO/SLI/SLA, error budgets, toil, on-call, runbooks, incidents. ([arbazkhan971/godmode](https://github.com/arbazkhan971/godmode/tree/HEAD/skills/reliability) · ★30)
- **reliability**: Run electromigration, IR-drop, ESD, latch-up, and aging (HCI/NBTI) analyses on a signed-off analog layout and sign off long-term reliability. Use when verifying that power/signal routing and devices survive the rated current, voltage, and lifetime, or re-validating after a custom-layout / circuit-design fix_request was serviced. Opens fix_requests routed to custom-layout (EM/IR) or circuit-design (ESD). ([hdl-tools/analog-chip-design-agents](https://github.com/hdl-tools/analog-chip-design-agents/tree/HEAD/plugins/reliability/skills/reliability) · ★26)
- **reliability**: Check whether an AI analysis answer is STABLE by running the same question several independent times and measuring what holds versus what drifts. Use when the user types "/reliability", or asks to "run this a few times", "is this answer stable / reliable", "check reliability of &lt;question&gt;", "does it give the same answer again", "run it N times and compare", or wants to know if a number can be trusted enough to act on. This is the cheapest eval and the only one that needs no answer key: it tells you about stability, not correctness.
